\widetilde{v}_{i}=\operatorname{Pval}(Q_{i})
. Choose
\mu\geq 0
such that
\alpha(w)(Q_{1})\subset Q_{2}
for any
w\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
with
\rho(w)\geq\mu
and such that there exists
w\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
with
\rho(w)=\mu
. Given
h\in H
, suppose that
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
is such that
\alpha(z)(h)\in Q_{1}
. If
w\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
with
\rho(w)=\rho(z)+\mu
then we see that

\alpha(w)(h)=\alpha(wz^{-1})(\alpha(z)(h))\in\alpha(wz^{-1})(Q_{1})\subset Q_{2}.

Thus
\inf\{\rho(w):\alpha(w)(h)\in Q_{2}\}\leq\rho(z)+\mu
. Taking the negative infimum over all such
z
yields
\widetilde{v}_{2}(h)\geq\widetilde{v}_{1}(h)-\mu
, and so
\widetilde{v}_{2}\succeq_{\rho}\widetilde{v}_{1}
, as desired.
∎

We now show that the map
\operatorname{Conf}
is also order-reversing, and consequently respects the equivalence relation on pseudo-valuations.

Lemma B.10
.

If
\widetilde{v}_{1},\widetilde{v}_{2}
are pseudo-valuations such that
\widetilde{v}_{1}\preceq_{\rho}\widetilde{v}_{2}
, then
\operatorname{Conf}(\widetilde{v}_{2})\preceq_{\rho}\operatorname{Conf}(\widetilde{v}_{1})
. In particular, if
\widetilde{v}_{1}\sim_{\rho}\widetilde{v}_{2}
, then
\operatorname{Conf}(\widetilde{v}_{1})\sim_{\rho}\operatorname{Conf}(\widetilde{v}_{2}).

Proof.

Set
Q_{i}=\operatorname{Conf}(\widetilde{v}_{i})
. Since
\widetilde{v}_{1}\preceq_{\rho}\widetilde{v}_{2}
, there exists a constant
K
such that
\widetilde{v}_{1}(h)\leq\widetilde{v}_{2}(h)+K
for all
h\in H
. It then follows from the property (PV3) applied to
\widetilde{v}_{2}
that
\alpha(z)(Q_{1})\subset Q_{2}
for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
such that
\rho(z)\geq K
. Thus
Q_{2}\preceq_{\rho}Q_{1}
by Lemma
B.8
. ∎

It follows that our maps
\operatorname{Conf}
and
\operatorname{Pval}
descend to maps between the equivalence classes of confining sets and equivalence classes of pseudo-valuations subordinate to
\rho
. We will now show that these maps are inverses, which will prove that they are order-reversing isomorphisms.
We first show the composition
\operatorname{Pval}\circ\operatorname{Conf}
is the identity on equivalence classes of pseudo-valuations.

Proposition B.11
.

If
\widetilde{v}
is a pseudo-valuation subordinate to
\rho
, then
\widetilde{v}\sim_{\rho}\operatorname{Pval}(\operatorname{Conf}(\widetilde{v}))
.

Proof.

Set
Q=\operatorname{Conf}(\widetilde{v})
and
\widetilde{u}=\operatorname{Pval}(Q)
.
We first show that
\widetilde{v}(h)=+\infty
if and only if
\widetilde{u}(h)=+\infty
. Let
\widetilde{v}(h)=+\infty
. Observe that
\widetilde{v}(\alpha(z)(h))=\rho(z)+\widetilde{v}(h)=+\infty
for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. It follows that
\operatorname{inf}\{\rho(z):\alpha(z)(h)\in Q\}=-\infty
, and thus
\widetilde{u}(h)=+\infty
. Conversely, suppose that
\widetilde{u}(h)=+\infty
. By the definition of
\widetilde{u}
, there is a sequence
\{y_{i}\}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{n}
with
\rho(y_{i})\to-\infty
such that
\alpha(y_{i})(h)\in Q
for all
i
. But then
\widetilde{v}(\alpha(y_{i})(h))=\rho(y_{i})+\widetilde{v}(h)\geq 0
for all
i
. As the
\rho(y_{i})
are arbitrarily negative (and
\rho
takes values only in
\mathbb{R}
), this is only possible if
\widetilde{v}(h)=+\infty
.

We now show that there is a constant
K_{0}
such that

\widetilde{u}(h)\leq\widetilde{v}(h)\leq\widetilde{u}(h)+K_{0}

for all
h\in H
, which will prove the result. Let
h\in H
. In light of the above, we may assume that both
\widetilde{u}(h),\widetilde{v}(h)
are finite values, for if
\widetilde{u}(h)=+\infty=\widetilde{v}(h)
, they satisfy the inequality for any constant
K_{0}
. Suppose that
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
such that
\alpha(z)(h)\in Q
. By definition
\widetilde{v}(\alpha(z)(h))\geq 0
, and therefore
\rho(z)+\widetilde{v}(h)\geq 0
and
\rho(z)\geq-\widetilde{v}(h).
Taking an infimum on the left-hand side over all such
z
gives us that

\operatorname{inf}\{\rho(z):\alpha(z)(h)\in Q\}\geq-\widetilde{v}(h).

Thus
\widetilde{u}(h)\leq\widetilde{v}(h)
.
To establish the other inequality, note that since the image of
\rho
in
\mathbb{R}
is either dense or cyclic, there exists
K_{0}>0
such that every point of
\mathbb{R}
is within
K_{0}
of a point of
\operatorname{Im}(\rho)
.
Choose
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
such that
0\leq\widetilde{v}(h)+\rho(z)\leq K_{0}
. Thus
0\leq\widetilde{v}(\alpha(z)(h))\leq K_{0}
so that
\alpha(z)(h)\in Q
and
\rho(z)\leq-\widetilde{v}(h)+K_{0}
. It then follows that
\widetilde{u}(h)\geq\widetilde{v}(h)-K_{0}
so that
\widetilde{v}(h)\leq\widetilde{u}(h)+K_{0}
. This completes the proof.∎

Next, we prove that the composition
\operatorname{Conf}\circ\operatorname{Pval}
is the identity on equivalence classes of confining subsets.

Proposition B.12
.

If
Q
is confining with respect to
\rho
, then
Q\sim_{\rho}\operatorname{Conf}(\operatorname{Pval}(Q))
.

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{v}=\operatorname{Pval}(Q)
and
Q^{\prime}=\operatorname{Conf}(\widetilde{v})
. Since
Q
is confining with respect to
\rho
, Definition
B.2
(a) yields that

q\in Q\Rightarrow\operatorname{inf}\{\rho(z):\alpha(z)(q)\in Q\}\leq 0\Rightarrow\widetilde{v}(q)\geq 0\Rightarrow q\in Q^{\prime}.
Thus
Q\subset Q^{\prime}
and hence
Q^{\prime}\preceq_{\rho}Q
. Conversely, let
q\in Q^{\prime}
. Then
\widetilde{v}(q)\geq 0
, and the definition of
\widetilde{v}
yields that
\alpha(z)(q)\in Q
for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
such that
\rho(z)>0
. Thus
Q\preceq_{\rho}Q^{\prime}
by Lemma
B.8
, completing the proof.
∎

Finally we are ready to put together the above results to prove Theorem
B.1
.

Proof of Theorem
B.1
.

By Lemmas
B.9
and
B.10
, the maps
\operatorname{Conf}
and
\operatorname{Pval}
descend to order-reversing maps on the equivalence classes of pseudo-valuations subordinate to
\rho
and confining subsets with respect to
\rho
, respectively. It follows from Propositions
B.11
and
B.12
that the maps are inverses of each other on the equivalence classes. Since both are order-reversing, they descend to inverse isomorphisms between the poset of pseudo-valuations and the opposite of the poset of confining subsets.
∎

We end this section with a discussion of the structure of a confining subset associated to a pseudo-valuation on
H
subordinate to
\rho
. In particular, we show that, up to equivalence of generating sets,
Q
is the union of a collection of cosets of a subgroup
K
of
H
, defined by

K=\{h\in H:\widetilde{v}(h)=+\infty\}.

The reader may check using conditions (PV2) and (PV3) that
K
is indeed a subgroup. Since
Q=\{h\in H:\widetilde{v}(h)\geq 0\}
, it is clear that
K\subset Q
. Let
T
be a transversal for
K
in
H
containing
0
. Let
T_{Q}\subseteq T
be the set of representatives of cosets of
K
that have non-empty intersection with
Q
, and let
\overline{K}=\displaystyle\bigcup_{g\in T_{Q}}(g+K)
.

Our goal is to prove the following.

Proposition B.13
.

Let
\widetilde{v}
be a pseudo-valuation on
H
subordinate to
\rho
, and let
Q=\operatorname{Conf}(\widetilde{v})
be the associated confining set. Then we have
[Q\cup Z_{\rho}]=\left[\overline{K}\cup Z_{\rho}\right].

If
K=0
, then the proposition is vacuous. However, it may yield interesting information if
K\neq 0
. We begin by proving some properties of the subgroup
K
.

Lemma B.14
.

If
\widetilde{v}
is a pseudo-valuation on
H
subordinate to
\rho
and
Q=\operatorname{Conf}(\widetilde{v})
, then
K
is a subgroup of
H
invariant under the action of
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and hence normal in
G
. Further,
K
is the (unique) largest such subgroup contained in
Q
.

Proof.

To see the invariance of
K
under the action of
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, observe that
\widetilde{v}(\alpha(z)(k))=\rho(z)+\widetilde{v}(k)=+\infty
for any
k\in K
and
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, as
\rho
only takes finite values in
\mathbb{R}
. Thus
\alpha(z)(k)\in K
for all
k\in K,z\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. The normality of
K
follows from its invariance under
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and the fact that
H
is abelian.

To prove the final statement, suppose for contradiction that
K^{\prime}
is a subgroup contained in
Q
that is invariant under the action of
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and such that
K^{\prime}\backslash K\neq\emptyset
. Then there exists an
h\in K^{\prime}
with
0\leq\widetilde{v}(h)<+\infty
. Since
K^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
–invariant,
\alpha(z)(h)\in K^{\prime}
for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. However,
\widetilde{v}(\alpha(z)(h))=\rho(z)+\widetilde{v}(h)
, and so choosing
z
with
\rho(z)
very negative shows there are elements in
K^{\prime}\subseteq Q
whose valuations are negative. This contradicts the definition of
Q
, and so
K^{\prime}\subseteq K
.
∎

The subgroup
K
can be very different in different cases. For instance, let
\varphi\in SL_{3}(\mathbb{Z})
be a matrix with irrational eigenvalues. If
G=\mathbb{Z}^{3}\rtimes_{\varphi}\mathbb{Z}
, then
K
has to be a
\varphi-
invariant subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
for any confining subset
Q
. If
Q
is a strictly confining subset, we must have
K=\{0\}
. On the other hand, if
Q=\mathbb{Z}^{3}
, then
K=\mathbb{Z}^{3}
. In the case of
G=\mathbb{R}^{3}\rtimes_{\varphi}\mathbb{Z}
, the subgroup
K
will be one of the invariant subspaces for the action of
\varphi
on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, depending on the choice of confining subset.

The next lemma shows that every strictly confining subset intersects one of the non-trivial cosets of
K
.

Lemma B.15
.

If
\displaystyle Q\cap\left(\bigcup_{g\in T\setminus\{0\}}g+K\right)=\emptyset
, then
Q=K=H
.

Proof.

We must have
Q=K
, as
K\subset Q
and
Q
does not intersect any non-trivial coset of
K
by assumption. Thus
Q
is a
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
–invariant subgroup of
H
. By Definition
B.2
(b), for every
h\in H
, there is a
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
such that
\alpha(z)(h)\in Q
, and hence
h\in\alpha(z^{-1})(Q)=Q
.∎

We are now ready to prove Proposition
B.13
.

Proof of Proposition
B.13
.

We first show that
\overline{K}\cup Z_{\rho}
is a generating set for
G=H\rtimes_{\alpha}\mathbb{Z}^{n}
. As
Z_{\rho}
generates
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, it suffices to show that
H
is contained in the subgroup generated by
\overline{K}\cup Z_{\rho}
.

Let
h\in H
. Then
h\in g+K
for some
g\in T
. By Definition
B.2
(b), there is a
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
such that
\alpha(z)(h)\in Q
. Hence
\alpha(z)(h)\in Q\cap(g^{\prime}+K)
for some
g^{\prime}\in T
. In particular,
g^{\prime}\in T_{Q}
and
h\in\alpha(z^{-1})(g^{\prime}+K)
, so
h
is contained in the subgroup generated by
\overline{K}\cup Z_{\rho}
.

To establish the equivalence of the generating sets, we will show that each generating set has uniformly bounded word length with respect to the other. First of all,
Q\subset\overline{K}
by definition of
T_{Q}
.

Conversely,
K\subset Q
by definition. If
Q
intersects the coset
g+K
non-trivially, we will show that this coset has uniformly bounded word length in
Q\cup Z_{\rho}
, which will prove the result. By assumption, there exists an element
q\in Q
of the form
q=g+k
with
k\in K
. For any other
k^{\prime}\in K
, write
g+k^{\prime}=q+(k^{\prime}-k)
. As
K
is a subgroup contained in
Q
, this implies that
g+k^{\prime}\in Q+Q
. If
z_{0}
is the element from Definition
B.2
(c), then
\alpha(z_{0})(g+k^{\prime})\in Q
. As
k^{\prime}
was arbitrary, it follows that the word length of any element of
g+K
with respect to
Q\cup Z_{\rho}
is at most
2\|z_{0}\|_{Z_{\rho}}+1
.
∎
